# Claim Ledger

Per-claim evidence register for the 10 Kasım project. Every load-bearing claim that has been
checked gets a row; claims not listed here are **unchecked transcript inheritance**.

**Evidence tiers** — the honest vocabulary:
- **P** — primary document or archive (the Nobel nomination record, the letter image, the TIME cover)
- **S** — scholarly secondary (university-press books, journal articles, TDV İslâm Ansiklopedisi, H-Net, researcher PDFs)
- **J** — journalism (Variety, Al Jazeera, Guardian, Hürriyet, BirGün…)
- **F** — fact-checker or research association (Malumatfuruş, Teyit, Honest History) — *their* work trusted, not replicated
- **T** — tertiary (Wikipedia, publisher/bookseller pages — fine for bibliographic data, weak for facts)
- **M** — assistant's training memory, not verified — must be resolved before print *(all prior M rows resolved 2026-08-03; see final table)*

**Status:** ✅ corroborated · 🎭 apocryphal-but-meaningful (reception data) · 🗣 circulating-unsourced ·
⚠️ single-source (needs corroboration) · ⏳ pending page-level check · ❓ open question

**Link-rot insurance (re-run 2026-10-29, 264 cited URLs):** 158 had Wayback snapshots, 65 saved fresh
via Save Page Now, 41 not archivable (Gallica, DOI redirects and publisher paywalls refuse the
archiver; the archiver also 404s two live repositories). Inventory: [`wayback_status.txt`](wayback_status.txt)
(`NONE` = no copy). Key sources the archiver cannot hold are banked as text in `sources/` (Sorby 2015;
Tezak thesis). Live check of the same 264: 198 OK on first pass; 32 bot-blocked (403, normal for
publishers); 7 Hansard links repaired to canonical form; 1 malformed Springer link flagged.

**Link-rot insurance (2026-08-03): complete — all 38 cited URLs have Wayback coverage.**
31 had existing snapshots (inventory: [`wayback_status.txt`](wayback_status.txt)); 6 were saved
fresh via Save Page Now (HTTP 200); the Honest History PDF's save endpoint 520s, but a 2024-06-29
snapshot already exists. The Nobel record — 403 on the live site — was read through its
2026-05-14 snapshot.

local OCR) and the Dutch East Indies press via Delpher. The round-3 card "Stockholm and Warsaw" was
merged into "Helsinki and Stockholm" and "Warsaw Compares Him to Washington".

## Census round 5 (2026-10-28/29) — primary archives + hostile hunt

Browser-grade tools (render/browse) opened Trove, NewspaperSG search, Gallica page search and the Hansard API.
Rule: browser-agent transcriptions are leads until a second retrieval confirms the text (one NewspaperSG
transcription was partly hallucinated and was caught by this rule). Main-agent spot checks: Hansard 0 hits
re-confirmed via API (control "genius": 709); Evening Post "A Good Dictator" quote confirmed verbatim;
Toynbee "I have not met Mustafa Kemal Pasha in the flesh" confirmed in the 1922 text (vault card corrected).

## Census round 9 (2026-10-31) — the İzmir plot in the Assembly

Five records, all read from the PDF text layer of the TBMM *Zabıt Ceridesi* and checked before merge (5 confirmed).
The Assembly did not sit between 10 June and 1 November 1926, so the İzmir plot (15/16 June) enters the open record
only with Kemal's opening speech of the fourth session; the chamber's answer is the congratulations motions of 3 November.
The 1926 text layer is OCR-degraded; quotes are de-hyphenated and obvious errors normalised, with the raw forms named in
the notes.

| Claim | Node | Status | Sources | Next step |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Kemal, opening the fourth session (1 Nov 1926, printed p. 2): the plot grieved him "not because it concerned our humble person, but because it showed a reactionary mentality" degenerate enough to attack the Assembly's dignity — the target framed as nation and Assembly, not the man | The Assembly Congratulates the Gazi after the İzmir Plot, 1 and 3 November 1926 | ✅ P | https://www5.tbmm.gov.tr/tutanaklar/TUTANAK/TBMM/d02/c027/tbmm02027001.pdf | — |

## Census round 8 (2026-10-30) — six-front primary sweep

All 98 records independently checked before merge: 92 confirmed, 3 paraphrase-ok, 1 secondary-only, 2 partial fixed
(a Pesti Hírlap reprint misattributed to its US reprinter; an over-read Action française fragment). La Croix 1924 quote
recovered at its true date (7 Mar). Seven note titles renamed to drop colons (Obsidian-incompatible).

## Source-check corrections (round 14, 2026-11-03)

| Record | Check verdict | Correction |
|---|---|---|
| reagan-1981-04-02 | partial | UCSB files it under "Statements by the Press Secretary": mediated_by White House Press Secretary; caveat says it is a statement in Reagan's name, not his spoken words |
| mohammad-reza-pahlavi-1980 | partial | Polarity admiring → witness (he reports his father's modelling on Atatürk; no verdict of his own) |
| gunther-1936 | partial | pp. 418–423 (1938 rev. ed.); intensity 3 → 2. The 1936 wording was first doubted, then confirmed by archive.org full-text search of the Harper 1936 first printing (follow-up, 3 Nov) |
| nicolson-1934 | paraphrase-ok | what_they_saw reworded ("deflates the legend" was the census's phrase, not his) |
| putin-2004-12-06 | paraphrase-ok | Kept with quote null; the Kremlin only paraphrases his honour-book entry |
| muhammad-asad-1954 | confirmed | Polarity conflicted → hostile on the checker's reading ("that petty masquerader", p. 297) |
| coudenhove-kalergi-1943 | confirmed | "far-sighted" line is pp. 126–127, not p. 125 |
| putin-2005-11-17 | partial | **Dropped**: Samsun named as the city of the liberation movement; no stated view of Atatürk |
| dewey-1924 | partial | **Dropped**: Dewey's 1924 articles never name Kemal; the praise is of "the new leaders" |
| west-1941 | partial | **Dropped**: "at least half Slav" is an aside about his eyes, not a view |

Vault correction: the inherited John Dewey note said he "praised Atatürk in his own writing"; his two 1924 *New Republic* articles never name him.

## Source-check corrections (round 19, 2026-11-03)

| Record | Check verdict | Correction |
|---|---|---|
| rafii-1935 | partial | **Dropped**: the hostile line is spoken by the fictional companion in his column; the narrator never endorses it |
| zaki-mubarak-1938 | partial | Polarity conflicted → hostile, intensity 1 (no praise on the page) |
| zaki-mubarak-1942 | partial | Polarity hostile → witness (comic mockery of a claim attributed to Kemal) |
| mandur-1944 | partial | Polarity admiring → instrumental |
| ahmad-ramzi-1946 | partial | Role reworded; Egyptian identity inferred only |
| tantawi-1935 | confirmed | Caveat: the subject is "the idolaters" |

Lead corrected: *al-Risala* nos. 836–838 ("Mustafa Kemal the Turkish leader") is Sforza's essay translated by Ahmad Ramzi, not a Khalil Mutran series.

**Seams closed.** Austria: all 6,853 Nationalrat/Bundesrat protocols listed by the parlament.gv.at Filter API (1918–2025) grepped; gaps are 173 image-only First-Republic Bundesrat sittings and 17 Nationalrat sittings of 1922–23 (Fraktur OCR unreliable) and ~35 provisional 2020–25 pages. European Parliament: ~40 Turkey sittings 2004–2023 rendered and read; undated debates (Oct 2005, mid-2007, Jul 2020 and a few others) unread. Canada: all 612 Commons and Senate debates volumes on Canadiana from the 13th Parliament (1917) to 1993 searched for "Ataturk" and "Kemal" — one gaze (Adamson 1943); other hits are index pages, Kemmel (1944), a Senate delegation's wreath (1970), a reading of the Gallipoli words (1992), a 1994 backbench attribution. HathiTrust: the full-text listing passes Cloudflare with a human click, but the in-volume search page renders blank even in Okan's own browser — dead end.

## Seam closed: Oireachtas (2026-11-04)

Oireachtas Open Data API per-sitting XML (data.oireachtas.ie, no WAF): Seanad 1922–2026, 5,189 sittings; Dáil 2014–2026,
1,308 sittings (Dáil 1919–2013 was covered by the Harvard Dataverse speeches dataset in round 23). No gaze kept:
Baxter (Seanad 1936, a list of dictators), Moffatt and Connor (Seanad 1999, attributions), airport-attack condolences
(2016), Byrne (Dáil 2025, an airport-name list), Broughan (Dáil 18 Dec 2019, "turned away from the great reforms of
Mustafa Kemal Ataturk": a legacy label, not staged for consistency with the Gosar and Weidenfeld drops). Yeats (Seanad
1922–28): no mention. Irish total: one gaze, John M. Kelly 1983.

## Residual risk across OCR-based sweeps (2026-11-04)

The Congress closing pass found two statements (Javits 1978, Pepper 1942) hidden by OCR garbles ("Ats.turk",
"Atattirk"). The Canadiana, archive.org full-text, Reichstag and Austrian First-Republic sweeps searched exact
spellings only, so passages where every spelling is garbled may be missed.

## Closing passes on the residual gaps (2026-11-04)

- **European Parliament 1996–2011 — closed.** The Europarl v7 corpus (statmt.org, 9,673 English sitting files) grepped
  for Atatürk/Kemal: 79 passages, none a new gaze from a notable voice. Otto von Habsburg (18 Sep 1998) only reports
  that "Azerbaijan's president" wrote "Atatürk, your Turks are back" in the Anıtkabir book (president unnamed);
  Swoboda (2003) and Tannock (2007, 2010) speak of Kemalism or "Ataturk's vision" as a label; Lambsdorff (2007) of
  Article 252. 2011–2023 rests on the ~40 rendered Turkey sittings (round 24a).
- **Canada, OCR-garble re-sweep.** All 612 Canadiana volumes re-searched for Mustapha, Mustafa, Angora, Attaturk,
  Atatiirk, Kemalist: complete (160 sessions, 612 volumes); new hits are place names (Jisr Mustafa Pasha, 1920), Angora goats/wool, other men named Mustafa/Mustapha (Sierra Leone's M. S. Mustapha 1960, UNEP's Mustafa Tolba, Egypt's Mustafa Kamel Helmi 1990); no new gaze. Closed.
- **Austrian First-Republic Bundesrat/Nationalrat (Fraktur) — closed.** Re-OCR with Tesseract deu_latf+Fraktur
  (tessdata_best) of all 193 image-only sittings (1920–34; ~7.3 million characters): 0 hits for Atatürk, Kemal (incl.
  OCR variants Remal/Kemäl/Kemai), Mustafa/Mustapha, Ghasi/Gazi, Kamal, Angora. No gaze.
- **Anıtkabir book 1953–90 — closed as a negative result.** Boran 2011 (thesis on the book, citing it page by page)
  records that high-level foreign visitors of the period "almost always only signed and dated".

## Census round 35 (2026-11-09): countries with no voices

The census held voices from about 80 countries; some 110 UN member states had none. Gemini (agy, memory only, no tools) was asked per country for notable people who spoke of Atatürk; a triage agent then checked the leads at source. Most answers were protocol-only Anıtkabir visits or nothing. A second seam: state-visit toasts printed in full by the visiting country's presidential archive (Kekkonen, Ahtisaari above).

## Independent re-check of rounds 34b–35 (2026-11-09)

Before release, every record added or changed in rounds 34b–35 was re-read by a second checker that had not written it: 22 Milliyet page-image records on fresh crops, 30 web- and book-source records by re-opening the source. All quotes were found in their sources. The checkers' corrections are in the records; the main ones:

| Record | What the re-check found | Result |
|---|---|---|
| haile-selassie-1967-03-04 | He was already in Ankara on 3 March (Milliyet 4 Mar 1967 p. 7); the wreath ceremony was on the 4th. The paper prints his reply about the wreath before the book entry. | Role and quote order corrected |
| william-crowe-1981-11-09 | The congress was organised by Boğaziçi University but held at the Atatürk Cultural Centre | Role corrected |
| joseph-luns-1986-05-19 | The same journal issue prints his English text (pp. 548 ff.); the record had quoted the Turkish translation and back-translated it | Now quoted in English |
| lord-kinross-1964 | Rustow misquotes Kinross: the book has 'could not endure to be loved too much', not 'bear' | Quoted from the book (search snippets) |
| ludwig-erhard-1963-11-10 | An unmarked omission between two sentences | Sentence restored |
| hanns-froembgen (now -1937) | The quoted p. 250 passage was written after December 1936; the German title given was wrong | Dated to the 1937 English edition; title removed |
| jalal-talabani-2008-03-07-book, frank-walter-steinmeier-2024-04-24 | 'State visit' not supported (a working visit; an official visit) | Roles corrected |
| saddam-hussein-1977-book | No independent record of a 1977 visit; the editor's name is not in the article | Verdict `secondary-only`; caveat says so |
| irfan-orga-1950 | The source link was a catalogue search, not the text | Now cites the Eland edition |
| macarthur-1953 (now -11-10), muammar-gaddafi-1981 (now -1981-05) | Dates: the message opens 'Today, 10 November'; the paper gives no date for Gaddafi's message. Neither page says 'telegram' | Dates and `artifact` corrected, records renamed |
| nehru, bourguiba, chiang-kai-shek (1963), helmut-kohl-1993 | Spelling and punctuation as printed ('bizmezliği', 'eğemenliğinin', 'Cumhutiyetinin'; straight quotes) | Restored as printed |
| tito-1954-04-13 | The page prints 'büyük, Atatürkün': with the comma, 'great' goes with the nation's strength, not with Atatürk | Quote, translation and summary follow the page; the caveat notes the comma may be a slip |
| adenauer-1954-03-18, mohammad-reza-pahlavi-1956-05-16 | 'âsil', 'dahî' as printed; he laid a bouquet, not a wreath; the certificate was presented by Mayor Orhan Eren, not the governor | Corrected |

Also: 36 records whose quoted text is a rendering of a foreign speaker's words (a Turkish paper's version, or a published translation) now carry `quote.is_translation: true` (the exhibition labels such text 'As printed'); every `summary` is 25 words or fewer; the two round-sweep notes in `vault/Events/` were regenerated from the corrected records.

**Added after the re-check (9 Nov 2026):** Mustafa Zein, Lebanese ambassador in Ankara, on his Arabic book about Atatürk: "Gerçek bir dahinin ne olduğunu göstermek istedim" (Milliyet 10 Nov 1983 p. 5; read by two readers). The 10 Nov 1978 issue has no foreign voice: its UNESCO item is a draft resolution with no named speaker.

## Census round 36 (2026-11-10): before 1919

The archive began in July 1919 because nobody had looked earlier. One search for texts that existed before 19 May 1919 (letters, despatches, newspapers, a wartime book) found fifteen candidates; a second reader then re-opened every source. None was contradicted. Eleven are entered as records, four are kept as leads in [`candidates/before-1919.md`](candidates/before-1919.md).

| Record | Date | What was read | Verdict | Corrected at the second read |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| `alvarez-1908-10` | Oct 1908 | Simon 1980 (Belleten 44/173), pp. 75–77, page images; F.O. 195/2271 not seen | secondary-only | He is named only 'Adjutant Kemal Bey'; dated to the month because the split between two despatches rests on Simon's footnotes |
| `ahmed-izzet-pasha-1913-02-22` | 22 Feb 1913 | Aktepe 1974 (Belleten 38/150), pp. 283–285, 288, page images | confirmed | Date is 22 Feb, not Aktepe's bracketed 23; wording taken from the print, not the web text; he argues against removing them, and the 'şübban' sentence covers both factions |
| `reichspost-1915-05-20` | 20 May 1915 | scan crops of p. 16 (ANNO) | confirmed | The voice is the paper carrying agency copy; 'Fifth Army headquarters' was an inference and is dropped |
| `liman-von-sanders-1915-09-30` | 30 Sep 1915 | facsimile of the handwritten letter (Belleten 32/128, plates) | confirmed | None; read line by line twice |
| `tasvir-i-efkar-1915-10-29` | 29 Oct 1915 | photograph of the front page | confirmed | Occasion added: the war anniversary issue |
| `erich-prigge-1916` | 1916 | page images of the 1916 printing | confirmed | None |
| `neue-freie-presse-1916-08-14` | 14 Aug 1916 | scan crops of p. 6 (ANNO) | confirmed | None |
| `dimitri-atschkow-1916-09-09` | 9 Sep 1916 | scan crops of p. 2 (ANNO) | confirmed | Speaker not identified beyond the paper's description; dateline only half visible |
| `enver-pasha-1917-09-29` | 29 Sep 1917 | İğdemir 1969 (Belleten 33), pp. 509–510 and facsimile | confirmed | First printing has 'kemal-i muvaffakıyetle' (the later book drops a word); it is a draft cipher telegram, not a letter |
| `carlo-sforza-1918-12-17` | 17 Dec 1918 | page images of DDI, serie 6, vol. I, pp. 317–318 | confirmed | The print says 'Ismail Kemal pascià'; the identification is the editors' |
| `buyuk-mecmua-1919-03-20` | 20 Mar 1919 | Arıkan 1989, pp. 607–612, page images; the magazine not seen | secondary-only | Not a fortnightly; the piece miscounts the anniversary |

Not entered: Minber of 17 Nov 1918 (he was a partner in the paper, and Enver Ziya Karal thought the biographical introduction may have been dictated by him), Ahmet Hulki in Minber of 19 Nov 1918 (known only in Karal's modernised rendering), Mahmut Şevket Paşa's diary for Feb 1913 (third-hand and modernised), and Ruşen Eşref's 1918 interview (the known wording is from the 1930 book). No teacher's or classmate's text from his school years was found. The viewer of the Austrian newspaper collection sits behind a browser check, so three records rest on scan crops returned by its search, not on the whole page.

In the exhibition, a record dated only to its year now follows that year's dated records.

## Page scans re-read (2026-11-10): the Italian-language American weeklies

The five Italian-language records from Chronicling America had been checked against the library's OCR text only. A contributor asked whether 'Piluski' in La Sentinella was an OCR slip, so each quote was read on the page scan (crops from the Library of Congress image service, tile.loc.gov; the image paths come from archived copies of the old Chronicling America pages).

| Record | Page | What the scan shows |
|---|---|---|
| `risorgimento-italiano-1922` | 21 Oct 1922, p. 1 | Verbatim |
| `risorgimento-italiano-1929` | 12 Jan 1929, p. 4 | 'sopra-tutto' is one word broken across a line; the OCR had 'sopra tutto'. Corrected to 'sopratutto' |
| `la-sentinella-bridgeport-1934` | 13 Sep 1934, p. 4 | Verbatim. 'Piluski' is the paper's own spelling of Piłsudski, not an OCR slip; the OCR's 'Tesperimento' is 'l'esperimento' on the page |
| `la-verita-waterbury-1935-05` | 3 May 1935, p. 2 | Verbatim |
| `la-verita-waterbury-1935-08` | 16 Aug 1935, p. 3 | Verbatim |
